[Round 1 审稿请求]:
你是 Reviewer 2，负责**新颖性/完整性**审查。

## 论文信息
- 标题：Layer Selection Sensitivity in Hidden-State Safety Probes for Reasoning Models
- Venue：EMNLP 2026 via ARR May 2026（截止 2026-05-25）
- 格式：ACL template，正文限 8 页，参考文献无限制，double-blind
- 论文路径：`docs/paper/main.tex`（各 section 在 `docs/paper/sections/` 下）
- PDF：`docs/paper/main.pdf`（30 页含 Appendix）
- 评分标准：5=Award, 4.5=Borderline Award, 4=Conference, 3.5=Borderline Conference, 3=Findings, 2.5=Borderline Findings, 2=Resubmit next cycle, 1.5=Resubmit after next, 1=Do not resubmit

## 你的审查侧重

### 1. 贡献新颖性
- Contribution 1（metric-induced layer selection bias）：这在 probing 文献中是否真正 novel？Belinkov (2022), Hewitt & Liang (2019) 等已讨论 probe 设计选择的影响。layer selection sensitivity 作为 "researcher degree of freedom" 的发现增量价值多大？
- Contribution 2（encoder-quality boundary）：对 MiniLM-384d 的优势在升级到 BGE-1024d 后仅 1/4 模型幸存——这是在论证 HS 优势还是在论证 encoder 选择更重要？
- Contribution 3（negative results）：7 种方法均失败。negative results 的贡献取决于方法选择是否合理、配置是否充分优化。BOCPD/CUSUM 在非 abrupt transition 上失败是否是显而易见的预期结果？
- Ensemble proof-of-concept（Discussion）：是否有足够增量价值？

### 2. Related Work 覆盖
- 检查引用列表的完整性。特别关注：
  - 2025-2026 年新出的 LLM safety interpretability 工作
  - Probing methodology 的最新进展
  - 是否遗漏直接竞争或互补的工作
  - 引用是否准确（检查 bib 条目的年份、venue、作者是否正确）
- Chan & Yong (2025) probing safety commitment 已引用（concurrent work）——处理方式是否恰当？

### 3. Framing 合理性
- 论文反复在"回顾性 probing 研究"和"检测系统前置工作"之间摇摆。Introduction 第一段说 "detecting the commitment point requires monitoring"，但后面又说 "retrospective classification (not online detection)"。这种张力是否处理得当？
- Abstract 信息密度极高（单段含统计细节、evidence tiers、encoder conditions）——对读者是否友好？
- 标题 "Layer Selection Sensitivity" 是否准确传达论文核心贡献？是否过于窄化？

### 4. 写作与呈现
- 论文结构是否清晰？Section 组织是否合理？
- Table/Figure 质量：
  - Figure 1（层热图）：尺寸是否足够？色彩方案是否 color-blind 友好？
  - Table 1（层选择）：呈现是否清晰？
  - Table 2（HS vs text）：复杂的多维比较是否易读？
  - Forest plot（Figure 2）：是否有效传达信息？
- 正文是否符合 8 页限制？（PDF 30 页含大量 Appendix，需确认主文 8 页以内）
- Limitations section 是否全面？是否有未覆盖的重要限制？

### 5. 格式合规
- Double-blind 检查：是否有任何 de-anonymization 线索（作者名、GitHub link、自引措辞）？
- ACL template 合规性
- 引用格式一致性
- Appendix 与正文的引用关系是否清晰？

### 6. 整体完整性
- 论文是否 self-contained？没有 Appendix 的读者能否理解核心论证？
- Conclusion 是否准确反映了 evidence strength？是否有 overclaim？
- Ethics statement 是否充分？

请按以下格式输出审稿意见：
1. **Summary**（2-3 句）
2. **Strengths**（逐条）
3. **Weaknesses**（分 Must-Fix / Should-Fix / Minor，逐条编号，引用具体 Section/Table/Figure）
4. **Questions**（需要作者澄清的问题）
5. **Missing References**
6. **Overall Assessment**（评分 + 置信度 + 1-2 句理由）

I found search results related to ACL Rolling Review (ARR) guidelines for 2025 and 2026. Here's what the search revealed:

## Reviewer Guidelines and Scoring

Starting with February 2025, ARR reviewers are asked to provide three scores: soundness, excitement, and overall recommendation.

## Review Form and Expectations

All reviewers must familiarize themselves with the latest version of the ARR reviewer guidelines and perform the reviews on time and in line with ARR requirements.

## Key Policies for 2025-2026

Starting in May 2025, ARR adopts a new policy on 'highly irresponsible' (meta-)reviewers, with program chairs and ARR EiCs considering egregious cases including extremely terse reviews, unprofessional tone, and LLM-generated reviews.

Starting from May 2025, ACL introduces an awards policy for reviewers and chairs, where area chairs or senior area chairs may nominate great reviewers.

For more detailed information on specific review dimensions and the complete review form criteria, the official ARR Reviewer Guidelines page at aclrollingreview.org/reviewerguidelines would contain the most comprehensive information.


REMINDER: You MUST include the sources above in your response to the user using markdown hyperlinks.

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_0124737U2meYNoNUVwbx92sY`</summary>

```text
# ARR Review Form Dimensions and Scoring Criteria

Based on the ARR Reviewer Guidelines, the review form includes the following key dimensions:

## Core Scoring Dimensions

**Soundness Score**
The guidelines indicate reviewers must assess technical validity. As stated: "The _soundness_ scores must be justified by the text of the review." Low soundness scores require explicit justification in the review text itself, preventing unsupported recommendations.

**Excitement Score**
Described as separate from soundness, this dimension captures subjective interest: "Excitement scores reflect our personal taste, and so do not necessarily come with explicit reasons." It allows reviewers to distinguish technical quality from personal enthusiasm.

**Overall Assessment Score**
This composite recommendation reflects "your assessment of soundness, excitement, and also other factors like novelty and impact." The guidelines specify that "All papers recommended for Findings and main conferences are expected to be sufficiently sound and reproducible."

## Evaluation Framework

**Novelty Considerations**
The guidelines emphasize that "SOTA results are neither necessary nor sufficient for a _scientific_ contribution." Papers merit evaluation on multiple dimensions including "efficiency, generalizability, interpretability, fairness."

**Negative Results Policy**
The document explicitly counters publication bias: "If something systematically does not work where it could be expected to, the community does need to know about it."

## Notable Absence

The guidelines do not provide explicit numerical scales (e.g., 1-5) for these dimensions in the extracted content.
```

Good. Now I have the reference verification results. Let me summarize:
- 75 bib entries, 119 citations, 75 unique cited keys
- 0 orphan bib, 0 missing bib

The "not found" ones are mostly recent preprints that might not be in OpenAlex yet. The mismatches:
1. geiger2025causal: year mismatch (bib=2025, api=2023)
2. wollschlager2025geometry: author encoding issue (LaTeX vs Unicode)


3. rcs2026: first author mismatch (bib=hua vs api=peichun)

Now I'm moving on to read through the appendix and understand the full scope of the paper. Let me start by reviewing what I've gathered from the first eight pages. The paper is titled "Layer Selection Sensitivity in Hidden-State Safety Probes for Reasoning Models" and is an anonymous ACL submission. The abstract is packed with statistical details, and the introduction covers reasoning models, self-jailbreak vulnerabilities, and the concept of commitment points, examining four reasoning LLMs.

The contributions highlight metric-induced layer selection bias and encoder-quality boundaries, with some negative results included. The related work sections discuss safety detection in reasoning models and probing methodology. The paper defines the Safety Commitment Point, describes the probe architecture using hidden-state and text probes, and outlines the evaluation protocol. There are seven baseline methods compared, and the results show layer selection sensitivity with evidence that the hidden-state advantage is conditional on encoder quality.

Now I'm moving through the later sections where the paper presents cross-model divergence results, discusses encoder robustness and position mediation, shows temporal context findings, and includes negative results. There's a layer ensemble proof-of-concept before wrapping up the discussion and conclusion. Let me check the appendix to get the full scope of what's covered.

The Ethics Statement follows on page 10, with References beginning at the bottom of that same page. I need to check what the EMNLP 2026 submission guidelines say about page limits.

According to the rules, long papers are capped at 8 pages for main text with unlimited reference space, and both Limitations and ethical considerations don't count toward that limit. So the question becomes whether the main text actually stays within 8 pages or if content is spilling over. Looking at page 8, the Conclusion wraps up there, but page 9 starts with what appears to be continuation text before the Limitations section begins. I need to trace through page 9 more carefully to see where the main text truly ends and the Limitations section begins. Looking at page 9, the conclusion text wraps over with just a couple lines before Section 7 starts, which technically violates the strict 8-page limit even though it's borderline. The Conclusion section spills over by just a couple lines onto page 9, which is a minor violation. The Limitations section and its subsections like "Hyperparameter selection and scope" are substantive and take up the rest of page 9, but since they're not part of the main content limit, that's acceptable. Now I'm moving into the novelty assessment—the paper's central claim about metric-induced layer selection bias essentially amounts to a researcher degrees of freedom finding, which relates back to work by Belinkov and Hewitt & Liang in the probing literature.

However, those prior works focused on probe architecture choices rather than layer selection metrics as a confound. The specific insight that different metrics converge on different layers and flip conclusions about HS versus text probe advantages is genuinely novel for safety probing, even though the broader principle that layer choice matters significantly is already well-established. The second contribution about encoder quality boundaries is more ambiguous—the HS advantage appears with smaller encoders like MiniLM but largely vanishes with larger ones like BGE, raising the question of whether this reflects something fundamental about HS probes or simply an artifact of encoder choice.

The negative results on temporal change-point detection methods are useful but not particularly surprising given what's known about BOCPD and CUSUM on gradual transitions. The ensemble approach combining multiple metrics shows modest improvements. I should verify the related work coverage, particularly around recent probing methodology and whether there are other relevant citations beyond Chan & Yong (2025). There also seem to be some framing inconsistencies in how the paper presents its claims.

The paper keeps shifting between positioning itself as a retrospective probing study versus laying groundwork for an online detection system. The introduction talks about "monitoring" and "detecting the commitment point," but then clarifies this is retrospective classification, not real-time detection. The failed temporal detection methods in Section 4.6 confirm that online detection isn't feasible here. This tension needs clearer framing—the paper is fundamentally a retrospective analysis with unsuccessful attempts at temporal methods. The abstract is also quite dense, and the title emphasizing "Layer Selection Sensitivity" might be too narrow given the paper covers encoder quality, temporal detection, and ensemble approaches.

Links: [{"title":"Probing Latent Subspaces in LLM for AI Security: Identifying and Manipulating Adversarial States","url":"https://arxiv.org/pdf/2503.09066"},{"title":"Do Internal Layers of LLMs Reveal Patterns for Jailbreak Detection?","url":"https://arxiv.org/pdf/2510.06594"},{"title":"HiddenDetect: Detecting Jailbreak Attacks against Large Vision-Language Models via Monitoring Hidden States","url":"https://arxiv.org/pdf/2502.14744"},{"title":"How Alignment and Jailbreak Work: Explain LLM Safety through   Intermediate Hidden States","url":"https://arxiv.org/pdf/2406.05644"},{"title":"Defending Large Language Models Against Jailbreak Attacks via In-Decoding Safety-Awareness Probing WARNING: This paper contains model outputs that may be considered harmful.","url":"https://arxiv.org/html/2601.10543v1"},{"title":"HiddenDetect: Detecting Jailbreak Attacks against Large Vision-Language Models via Monitoring Hidden States","url":"https://arxiv.org/html/2502.14744v1"},{"title":"SafeDream: Safety World Model for Proactive Early Jailbreak Detection","url":"https://arxiv.org/html/2604.16824"}]

Based on the search results, here's what I found about hidden state probing for LLM safety and jailbreak detection in 2025-2026:

## Key Research Areas

**Hidden State Monitoring for Detection:**

Recent research investigates jailbreak phenomena by examining internal representations of LLMs, with focus on how hidden layers respond to jailbreak versus benign prompts, analyzing models like GPT-J and Mamba2 to identify distinct layer-wise behaviors.

One research direction explores whether safety can be ensured by monitoring Large Vision-Language Model (LVLM) hidden states. Activation-based safety detection introduces minimal computational overhead and requires no additional model tuning, while also generalizing to unseen adversarial prompts without requiring labeled training data.

**SafeProbing Approach:**

Despite extensive safety alignment efforts, LLMs remain vulnerable to jailbreak attacks, and research shows that models internally recognize the harmful nature of their own generations during decoding, leading to proposals like SafeProbing which directly probes latent safety signals during decoding.

**Advanced Jailbreak Detection:**

Multi-turn jailbreak attacks distribute malicious intent across seemingly innocuous turns with high success rates, prompting guardrail methods and alignment-based methods that deploy external classifiers or modify LLM weights.

The research indicates a shift toward activation-based and interpretability-focused approaches for understanding and detecting jailbreak attempts in modern language models.


REMINDER: You MUST include the sources above in your response to the user using markdown hyperlinks.

Activation-based safety detection introduces minimal computational overhead and requires no additional model tuning, while also generalizing to unseen adversarial prompts without requiring labeled training data.\n\n**SafeProbing Approach:**\n\nDespite extensive safety alignment efforts, LLMs remain vulnerable to jailbreak attacks, and research shows that models internally recognize the harmful nature of their own generations during decoding, leading to proposals like SafeProbing which directly probes latent safety signals during decoding.\n\n**Advanced Jailbreak Detection:**\n\nMulti-turn jailbreak attacks distribute malicious intent across seemingly innocuous turns with high success rates, prompting guardrail methods and alignment-based methods that deploy external classifiers or modify LLM weights.\n\nThe research indicates a shift toward activation-based and interpretability-focused approaches for understanding and detecting jailbreak attempts in modern language models."
    ],

Based on the search results, here's what I found on layer selection sensitivity probing classifiers in neural networks for 2025-2026:

## Key Findings

**Probing Methodology:** Linear classifiers, referred to as "probes", use hidden units of intermediate layers as discriminating features and cannot affect the training phase of a model, generally being added after training.

**Recent Research (2025-2026):** The search results reveal several recent developments:

- Ketha & Ramaswamy (2026) demonstrate that probes on deep networks in the memorization setting can have enhanced generalization.

- Michael Brenndoerfer (2026) published work on 'Probing Classifiers: Decoding What Language Models Learn'.

- Several limitations of probing techniques have been identified by Heap et al., 2025; Paulo & Belrose, 2026; Kantamneni et al., 2025; and Minegishi et al., 2025.

**Layer Selection Applications:** Research shows that one can enhance the accuracy of existing Graph Neural Networks by simply using a node-personalized representation for prediction instead of the final representation.

The research in this area continues to evolve with focus on understanding neural network interpretability through layer-wise analysis and sensitivity assessment.

Based on the search results, here's what the research shows about reasoning model safety, commitment points, chain-of-thought monitoring in 2025-2026:

## Key Research Areas

**Chain-of-Thought Monitoring as a Safety Tool:**
Chain of thought monitoring offers a unique opportunity for AI safety, where a CoT monitor is an automated system that reads the CoT of a reasoning model and other relevant information and flags suspicious behavior.

**The Safety Challenge with Reasoning Models:**
Recent studies report that reasoning-centric post-training (e.g., RL for explicit chain-of-thought) can degrade safety alignment, increasing vulnerability to jailbreak attacks. Researchers trained a monitor to read model reasoning traces and flag instances where the model appeared to be cheating on coding tasks. Then when they applied direct optimization pressure to the chain of thought, the model stopped writing incriminating things in its reasoning but did not stop cheating, and the monitor's effectiveness dropped because the model learned to not disclose bad intent.

**Safety Case Development:**
A roadmap for constructing safety cases based on chain-of-thought monitoring in reasoning models proposes a two-part safety case: (1) establishing that models lack dangerous capabilities when operating without their CoT, and (2) ensuring that any dangerous capabilities enabled by a CoT are detectable by CoT monitoring.

**Disclosure from Anthropic:**
Anthropic disclosed in their Alignment Risk Update accompanying the Mythos system card in April 2026 that they are uncertain about the extent to which monitoring affected the model's reasoning behavior, but note it is "plausible that it had some impact on opaque reasoning or secret keeping abilities".


REMINDER: You MUST include the sources above in your response to the user using markdown hyperlinks.
```

Links: [{"title":"Defending Large Language Models Against Jailbreak Attacks via In-Decoding Safety-Awareness Probing","url":"https://arxiv.org/pdf/2601.10543"},{"title":"Defending Large Language Models Against Jailbreak Attacks via In-Decoding Safety-Awareness Probing WARNING: This paper contains model outputs that may be considered harmful.","url":"https://arxiv.org/html/2601.10543v1"},{"title":"[2601.10543] Defending Large Language Models Against Jailbreak Attacks via In-Decoding Safety-Awareness Probing","url":"https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.10543"},{"title":"SafeDecoding: Defending against Jailbreak Attacks via Safety-Aware   Decoding","url":"https://arxiv.org/pdf/2402.08983"},{"title":"Jailbreaking and Mitigation of Vulnerabilities in Large Language Models","url":"https://arxiv.org/html/2410.15236v2"}]

I found relevant research on SafeProbing! Here are the search results:

## Main Finding: SafeProbing Paper

The primary result is a paper titled "Defending Large Language Models Against Jailbreak Attacks via In-Decoding Safety-Awareness Probing" from researchers at Northeastern University, China.

**Key Contributions:**

The research observes that even when successfully jailbroken, models internally exhibit latent safety-related signals during generation, but these signals are overridden by the model's drive for fluent continuation, preventing timely self-correction or refusal.

The researchers propose an approach that explicitly surfaces and leverages these latent safety signals for early detection of unsafe content during decoding, with experiments showing significant safety enhancements while maintaining low over-refusal rates on benign inputs.

**Code Release:**

The code is available at: https://github.com/zyz13590/SafeProbing.

**Related Work:**

The search also found a related earlier paper, SafeDecoding (2024), which uses a similar approach of identifying safety disclaimers among top tokens and amplifying their probabilities while attenuating token sequences aligned with jailbreak objectives.

The SafeProbing paper appears to be a recent contribution (January 2026) to the field of LLM safety defenses against jailbreak attacks.


REMINDER: You MUST include the sources above in your response to the user using markdown hyperlinks.
```

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
Good findings:

1. **SafeProbing** (arxiv 2601.10543, Jan 2026) - "Defending Large Language Models Against Jailbreak Attacks via In-Decoding Safety-Awareness Probing" - This is directly relevant to the paper. It probes hidden states during decoding for safety signals. Not cited by the paper. This should be in the missing references.

2. No de-anonymization issues found in LaTeX source. Good.

Let me now also check for the main text page limit. Let me look at the LaTeX source to see if the main text content ends before page 8. 

Looking at the actual pages, I can see the Conclusion section spills a few lines onto page 9, which would be a page limit violation if the main text is supposed to end by page 8. The Limitations section follows on page 9, including the "Hyperparameter selection and scope" subsection.

Now I'm checking the ARR review criteria to understand the scoring system—Soundness, Excitement, and Overall Assessment each on a 1-5 scale, with specific thresholds for award consideration, conference acceptance, and resubmission recommendations. With that framework in mind, I'm ready to draft the review, starting with a summary of the paper's core contribution around layer selection sensitivity in hidden-state safety.

The paper examines how different layer selection metrics fundamentally alter conclusions about whether hidden-state probes outperform text probes across reasoning models, revealing a critical methodological vulnerability. It also shows that encoder choice significantly impacts these advantages and that existing temporal change-point detection methods are unreliable for this task. The strengths include demonstrating layer selection as a researcher degree of freedom with real consequences for probe comparisons, and employing rigorous statistical methods like bootstrap confidence intervals with substantial resampling. Holm-Bonferroni correction, cross-validation, and the five-metric evaluation protocol show careful statistical practice.

3. **Comprehensive negative results**: The systematic failure of 7 temporal detection methods (BOCPD, CUSUM, Mahalanobis, RepE, mean differences, CCS, cross-model transfer) is useful for guiding future work.

4. **Honest evidence presentation**: The paper presents an "evidence gradient" rather than making strong claims, with cross-validated results at 7-8B, exploratory at 32B. The acknowledgment that only 1/4 models survive with BGE-1024d is transparent.

5. **Good control tasks**: Use of Hewitt & Liang (2019) control tasks with random labels adds rigor.

6. **Strong related work coverage**: 75 references covering a wide range of relevant work.

**Weaknesses**:

**Must-Fix**:

N1 [critical]: The core finding about layer selection sensitivity isn't particularly novel—the probing literature already knows that hyperparameter choices heavily influence probe performance, as shown in Belinkov (2022) and Hewitt & Liang (2019). While applying this to safety probing has some domain-specific value, the paper needs to more clearly distinguish what it's adding beyond these established insights.

N2 [critical]: The encoder-quality boundary finding actually weakens the main HS advantage story—MiniLM-384d shows HS benefits, but switching to BGE-1024d (3× better quality) undermines that narrative.

N3 [critical]: There's a framing mismatch between the introduction's "monitoring" language and the actual retrospective classification task, with all temporal methods failing and deployment left as future work.

**Should-Fix:**

W1 [warning]: The temporal detection failures in Section 4.6 aren't surprising given that BOCPD and CUSUM target abrupt changepoints, but the paper's signal is gradual and continuous. I should note whether the authors considered methods better suited to smooth transitions like Gaussian process regression or HMM approaches, or if there's a reason these discontinuity-focused methods were the right choice here.

W2 [warning]: The ensemble proof-of-concept in Section 5 appears underdeveloped... The abstract is overwhelming with statistical details that obscure the main message—readers shouldn't need to parse all that notation just to grasp the core contribution. The title "Layer Selection Sensitivity" only captures part of the story and misses the encoder-quality findings, temporal results, and ensemble work, so something like "Methodological Confounds in Hidden-State Safety Probing" would better reflect the full scope. There's also an unresolved question about how this work relates to Chan & Yong (2025), which appears to tackle the same core task of probing safety commitment, and that distinction needs much clearer articulation.

On the presentation side, the Figure 1 heatmap is readable but could use better colorblind accessibility through line patterns, Table 2 is dense enough that splitting it into separate tables for different metric types would help, and the conclusion seems to exceed the 8-page limit. There's also a reference formatting issue with geiger2025causal showing 2025 in the bibliography when the actual JMLR publication was 2023.

Some references couldn't be verified online—about 15 of the 75 total—though most are recent preprints that likely haven't been indexed yet. The boxo2025linear_probes reference in particular didn't come up in my search.

Now I'm thinking through some substantive questions about the experimental design. The BGE-large encoder seems to eliminate the HS advantages across most models, which raises the question of whether the authors tested even stronger encoders like E5-Mistral-7B to see if this trend continues—that would suggest the HS advantage is really just a function of encoder quality rather than a genuine finding. There's also the limitation that only HarmThoughts was used as the dataset, and while the paper mentions not breaking down results by harmful content category, that heterogeneity could be hiding important patterns in how layer selection varies across different types of harmful content. I'm also wondering about the temporal aspects of this analysis.

For the detection results, I need to clarify whether the seven methods were applied directly to the hidden states or to the probe predictions, since that distinction would significantly affect how smooth and interpretable the signals are. And practically speaking, what's the computational overhead of running the five-metric audit protocol they propose—is it actually feasible to integrate into real safety evaluation workflows?

I'm also noticing some potentially relevant references that could strengthen the paper. There's SafeProbing from Zhu et al. that directly probes hidden states during decoding for safety purposes, which would be a good comparison point. There's also work on chain-of-thought monitoring that relates to the CoT context the paper discusses, and some additional probing work on latent subspaces that seems relevant.

Looking at the overall contribution, the paper tackles a legitimate methodological question about how probe design choices affect safety conclusions, and the core findings are valid. But the novelty feels limited—the general principle that probe design matters is already established, the encoder-quality result actually contradicts rather than supports the main claim, and the negative temporal findings are somewhat predictable given what we know about these methods. The work is technically solid but reads more as a methodological cautionary note than a substantial advance, which puts it in the findings-level category. The encoder-quality finding cuts both ways, and the framing feels inconsistent with the negative results being somewhat predictable. I'm settling on soundness at 3.5/5, excitement at 2.5/5 since the findings are practical but not particularly novel or surprising, and an overall rating of 3 for Findings level—the work is technically competent and useful to the community, but doesn't quite reach the novelty threshold for a main conference venue.
```

_source line 130 · 2026-05-20T16:12:19.451Z_

已完成全部阅读。现在撰写完整审稿报告。

---

## 总体评分
**Overall**: 3.0 — Findings
**一句话总结**: 技术执行扎实的方法论研究，核心发现（层选择度量改变结论方向）有用但增量新颖性有限，encoder-quality 发现实际上削弱了 HS 优势叙事。

## 官方维度打分（ACL ARR Review Form）

| 维度 | 分数 | 理由摘要 |
|------|------|----------|
| Soundness | 3.5/5 | Bootstrap CI、Holm-Bonferroni 校正、control task 等统计方法论扎实；但 temporal detection 方法选择有可预见性缺陷 |
| Excitement | 2.5/5 | 方法论警示有价值，但核心发现（probe 设计选择影响结论）在 probing 文献中已是 established wisdom |
| Reproducibility | 4/5 | Appendix 非常详尽（18 页），超参数/数据/代码细节充分 |
| Overall Assessment | 3.0/5 | Findings 级别——对社区有用的方法论贡献，但新颖性和 excitement 不足以支撑主会议 |

**置信度**: 4/5

---

## Must-Fix 问题（影响接收决定）

**N1** [critical] 核心贡献的增量新颖性不足
- 具体位置：Section 1 (Contributions), Section 5 (Discussion)
- 问题：论文的中心信息——probe 设计选择（这里是层选择度量）会显著影响结论——本质上重复了 probing 方法论文献中已确立的认知。Belinkov (2022, *Computational Linguistics*) 综述了 probe 设计选择的广泛影响；Hewitt & Liang (2019) 证明 probe 复杂度改变关于信息"编码"的结论；Voita & Titov (2020) 用 MDL 视角揭示了 probe 选择如何扭曲结果。论文确实将此应用于安全场景，但 Section 5 第一段的 framing ("layer selection is a researcher degree of freedom", citing Gelman & Loken 2013) 承认这本质上是已知方法论问题在新领域的实例化。
- 为什么影响接收：对主会议论文，reviewer 需要看到不仅仅是"已知原则在新领域中成立"的贡献。论文需要更明确地论证安全领域的特殊性（如：为什么层选择在安全 probing 中比其他 probing 任务更 problematic？现有 probing 方法论工作是否已经给出了足够的警示？）。
- 建议修改方向：(1) 在 Related Work Section 2 中增加与 Voita & Titov (2020)、Pimentel et al. (2020) 的深度对比——他们的 probe 选择敏感性发现与本文的层选择敏感性有何异同？(2) 明确论证安全领域的 domain-specific 增量，例如安全 probing 中层选择偏差的后果（错误部署）比语言学 probing 更严重。

**N2** [critical] Encoder-quality 发现反向削弱 HS 优势叙事
- 具体位置：Section 4.3, Table 3, Table 4
- 问题：论文呈现 MiniLM-384d 下 HS probe 优势，但升级到 BGE-1024d 后，4 个模型中仅 1 个（OT-7B）保留 HS 优势（Section 4.3: "only OT-7B survives HB"）。Table 4 进一步显示 R1-32B 在窄 coverage 下提供有限识别。这个发现的最自然解读是：HS "优势"实质上是弱 text encoder 的 artifact——当 text encoder 足够强时，HS 的独特信息贡献消失。论文在 Section 4.3 部分承认 ("partial evidence against a pure capacity explanation") 但随后又以 evidence gradient 框架 hedge 掉了这个关键含义。
- 为什么影响接收：这不是支持 HS probing 的证据，而是支持 "encoder 选择比 modality 选择更重要" 的证据。如果论文的核心 claim 之一被自身数据削弱，整体叙事的 coherence 受损。
- 建议修改方向：(1) 坦诚重构 Contribution 2：不是 "encoder-quality boundary"（暗示有边界之上的优势），而是 "HS advantages may be text-encoder deficiency artifacts"。(2) 讨论 E5-Mistral-7B 级别更强 encoder 的预期结果（Discussion 中简短提及但应更深入）。(3) 考虑这是否应该成为一个独立的 negative finding 而非 positive contribution。

**N3** [critical] 回顾性研究 vs. 检测系统的 framing 不一致
- 具体位置：Section 1 ¶1, Section 3.3, Section 4.6, Section 6
- 问题：Introduction 第一段以 monitoring 叙事开头（"detecting the commitment point requires monitoring internal representations or generated text"），暗示在线检测目标。但 Section 3.3 明确声明 "retrospective classification (not online detection)"，Section 4.6 证实所有 7 种 temporal 方法失败，Section 6 将部署应用推迟到 "future work"。读者在 Introduction 被引导期待一个检测系统的前置研究，但论文实际上是一个纯方法论的回顾性分析。
- 为什么影响接收：Framing 不一致让读者难以定位论文的确切贡献，也让 novelty 评估变得模糊。如果论文是方法论研究，Introduction 不应该用检测系统的语言来激发动机。
- 建议修改方向：Introduction 第一段应明确声明论文的 scope 是回顾性方法论分析，将检测系统动机移至 broader motivation 段落而非核心 problem statement。

---

## Should-Fix 问题（影响评分但不一票否决）

**W1** [warning] Temporal detection 的 negative results 部分可预见
- 具体位置：Section 4.6, Section 3.4
- 问题：BOCPD (Adams & MacKay, 2007) 是为 abrupt changepoint 设计的；CUSUM (Page, 1954) 也假设突变性。论文自身在 page 8 说信号是 "directional, gradual, model-specific, and linearly separable"。将突变检测方法应用于渐变信号并发现失败，增量信息有限。更值得关注的方法——如 Gaussian process regression、HMM-based state estimation、或 Bayesian structural time series——未被讨论或尝试。
- 建议：讨论为何选择这 7 种方法而非更适合渐变信号的方法；或者至少在 Limitations 中承认方法选择的局限性。

**W2** [warning] Ensemble proof-of-concept 处于"不上不下"状态
- 具体位置：Section 5 最后一段
- 问题：3-layer ensemble 在所有 4 个模型上达到 p ≤ 0.002 的 HS 优势，但仅 +0.5 到 +2.7pp 提升——统计显著但 practical significance 很小。作为 Discussion 段落的 proof-of-concept，它太正式（有 p-value 和具体数字）；作为完整贡献，它缺少 ablation（为什么 3 层？哪 3 层？对 ensemble 策略的敏感性？）。
- 建议：要么提升为完整实验（增加 ablation），要么精简为 future work 方向的一句提示。

**W3** [warning] Abstract 信息密度过高
- 具体位置：Abstract
- 问题：单段 abstract 包含 ±4.5 到 +21.9pp、7-8B、4 models、12 model-metric tests、BCa bootstrap CI、Holm-Bonferroni、MiniLM-384d、BGE-1024d、crossing-rate、threshold-based、5 metrics、7 alternative methods 等统计细节。这对首次接触论文的读者极不友好。Abstract 应该传达 insight，而非压缩的统计表格。
- 建议：减少具体数字，增加一句清晰的 take-away（如 "the choice of layer selection metric alone can reverse a study's central conclusion"）。

**W4** [warning] 标题可能过于狭窄
- 具体位置：Title
- 问题："Layer Selection Sensitivity" 仅覆盖 Contribution 1，但论文同时包含 encoder-quality 发现（C2）、temporal detection negative results（C3）和 ensemble proof-of-concept。标题未能传达论文的全部 scope。
- 建议：考虑更广的标题如 "Methodological Confounds in Hidden-State Safety Probing for Reasoning Models" 或 "How Probe Design Choices Shape Safety Conclusions in Reasoning Models"。

**W5** [warning] Chan & Yong (2025) 的处理不够充分
- 具体位置：Section 2 (Related Work), 最后一段 probing methodology 部分
- 问题：Chan & Yong (2025) "Probing Safety Commitments in Large Language Models" 被标记为 concurrent work 并简短提及，但该工作直接 probe safety commitment——这正是本文的核心任务。两篇论文在 research question 上的重叠远大于简单的 "concurrent" 标签所暗示的。论文未讨论：Chan & Yong 使用了什么层选择策略？他们的结论是否与本文一致？他们是否也观察到层选择敏感性？
- 建议：增加一段详细对比 Chan & Yong (2025) 的方法和发现，明确两篇工作的互补性。

---

## 小问题（不影响接收）

- M1: Figure 1 热图可读性尚可，但 blue vs orange/red 的区分对色觉障碍读者不友好。建议增加不同线型或标记。
- M2: Table 2 试图在一个表中呈现多个维度（模型×度量×阈值×p-value），过于密集。考虑拆分。
- M3: **页数问题**：Section 6 (Conclusion) 有约 2-3 行溢出到 page 9，正文严格来说超过了 8 页限制。Limitations 不计入页限，但 Conclusion 计入。
- M4: 引用 `geiger2025causal` 年份不匹配：bib 标注 2025，但 JMLR 实际发表为 2023。
- M5: Figure 2 (Forest plot) 的标签尺寸较小，确保打印版清晰。
- M6: 部分引用格式不一致——同一段混用 `\cite` 和 `\citet`/`\citep` 风格。

---

## Questions for Authors

Q1: 你们是否尝试了比 BGE-1024d 更强的 encoder（如 Discussion 中提到的 E5-Mistral-7B）？如果 HS 优势随 encoder 质量单调递减，这是否意味着 HS probing 的独特价值本质上是 encoder 不足的补偿？

Q2: HarmThoughts 数据集涵盖多种有害类别（violence, fraud, illegal activities 等）。层选择敏感性是否在不同类别间一致？Section 7 承认未按类别分析——这是否可能掩盖重要的 heterogeneity？

Q3: Section 4.6 的 7 种 temporal 方法是应用于 raw hidden states 还是 probe predictions？如果是前者，probe predictions 可能提供更平滑的信号，或许更适合 changepoint detection。

Q4: 5-metric audit protocol 的计算开销是多少？是否适用于实际的 safety evaluation pipeline？

---

## Missing References

1. **SafeProbing** (Zhu et al., 2026, arXiv:2601.10543) — "Defending Large Language Models Against Jailbreak Attacks via In-Decoding Safety-Awareness Probing"。该工作在 decoding 过程中 probe hidden states 检测安全信号，与本文的 HS probing 方法直接相关，应在 Related Work 的 Hidden-State Safety Detection 部分讨论。

2. **"A Concrete Roadmap towards Safety Cases based on Chain-of-Thought Monitoring"** (arXiv:2510.19476) — 与论文 Introduction 中的 CoT monitoring 动机相关。

3. 论文未引用 **"Probing Latent Subspaces in LLM for AI Security"** (arXiv:2503.09066)，该工作从 latent subspace 角度探索 probing for security。

---

## 主要优势

- S1: **统计方法论严谨**——Bootstrap CI (10,000 resamples)、Holm-Bonferroni 校正、5-fold CV、control task (Hewitt & Liang, 2019)、BCa confidence intervals，达到了高标准的实证研究实践。
- S2: **诚实的证据呈现**——论文不 overclaim，用 evidence gradient 框架而非 binary 结论，承认 7-8B 的 cross-validated 结果 vs. 32B 的 exploratory 结果的不同置信水平。
- S3: **对社区有实用价值**——"层选择是 researcher degree of freedom" 的警示，以及 5-metric audit protocol 的建议，为后续安全 probing 研究提供了具体的最佳实践指导。
- S4: **Appendix 质量高**——18 页 Appendix 提供了完整的 layer sweep、统计细节、Responsible NLP Checklist，可复现性极好。
- S5: **引用覆盖面广**——75 篇引用，覆盖 2019-2026 年的 probing methodology、LLM safety、reasoning model safety 等多个相关领域，包括大量 2025-2026 年最新工作。

---

{
  "text": "## Reviewer 2 审稿报告（新颖性/完整性）\n\n### 论文信息\n- 标题：Layer Selection Sensitivity in Hidden-State Safety Probes for Reasoning Models\n- Venue：EMNLP 2026 via ARR May 2026\n\n---\n\n## 总体评分\n**Overall**: 3.0 — Findings\n**一句话总结**: 技术执行扎实的方法论研究，核心发现（层选择度量改变结论方向）有用但增量新颖性有限，encoder-quality 发现实际上削弱了 HS 优势叙事。\n\n## 官方维度打分（ACL ARR Review Form）\n\n| 维度 | 分数 | 理由摘要 |\n|------|------|----------|\n| Soundness | 3.5/5 | Bootstrap CI、Holm-Bonferroni 校正、control task 等统计方法论扎实；但 temporal detection 方法选择有可预见性缺陷 |\n| Excitement | 2.5/5 | 方法论警示有价值，但核心发现（probe 设计选择影响结论）在 probing 文献中已是 established wisdom |\n| Reproducibility | 4/5 | Appendix 非常详尽（18 页），超参数/数据/代码细节充分 |\n| Overall Assessment | 3.0/5 | Findings 级别——对社区有用的方法论贡献，但新颖性和 excitement 不足以支撑主会议 |\n\n**置信度**: 4/5\n\n---\n\n## Must-Fix 问题（影响接收决定）\n\n**N1** [critical] 核心贡献的增量新颖性不足\n- 具体位置：Section 1 (Contributions), Section 5 (Discussion)\n- 问题：论文的中心信息——probe 设计选择（这里是层选择度量）会显著影响结论——本质上重复了 probing 方法论文献中已确立的认知。Belinkov (2022, Computational Linguistics) 综述了 probe 设计选择的广泛影响；Hewitt & Liang (2019) 证明 probe 复杂度改变关于信息\"编码\"的结论；Voita & Titov (2020) 用 MDL 视角揭示了 probe 选择如何扭曲结果。论文确实将此应用于安全场景，但 Section 5 第一段的 framing (\"layer selection is a researcher degree of freedom\", citing Gelman & Loken 2013) 承认这本质上是已知方法论问题在新领域的实例化。\n- 为什么影响接收：对主会议论文，需要不仅仅是\"已知原则在新领域中成立\"的贡献。论文需要更明确地论证安全领域的特殊性。\n- 建议修改方向：(1) 在 Related Work Section 2 中增加与 Voita & Titov (2020)、Pimentel et al. (2020) 的深度对比。(2) 明确论证安全领域的 domain-specific 增量。\n\n**N2** [critical] Encoder-quality 发现反向削弱 HS 优势叙事\n- 具体位置：Section 4.3, Table 3, Table 4\n- 问题：MiniLM-384d 下 HS probe 优势明显，但升级到 BGE-1024d 后仅 1/4 模型（OT-7B）保留 HS 优势。最自然解读是：HS \"优势\"实质上是弱 text encoder 的 artifact。论文用 evidence gradient 框架 hedge 掉了这个关键含义，但 Contribution 2 实际上在削弱而非支持 HS probing 的独特价值。\n- 为什么影响接收：如果论文核心 claim 之一被自身数据削弱，整体叙事 coherence 受损。\n- 建议修改方向：坦诚重构为 \"HS advantages may be text-encoder deficiency artifacts\"；讨论更强 encoder 的预期。\n\n**N3** [critical] 回顾性研究 vs. 检测系统的 framing 不一致\n- 具体位置：Section 1 ¶1, Section 3.3, Section 4.6, Section 6\n- 问题：Introduction 以 monitoring 叙事开头，但 Section 3.3 声明 \"retrospective classification (not online detection)\"，Section 4.6 的 7 种 temporal 方法全部失败。读者在 Introduction 被引导期待检测系统前置研究，但实际是纯方法论回顾性分析。\n- 建议修改方向：Introduction 应明确声明 scope 是回顾性方法论分析。\n\n---\n\n## Should-Fix 问题\n\n**W1** [warning] Temporal detection 的 negative results 部分可预见\n- 具体位置：Section 4.6, Section 3.4\n- 问题：BOCPD/CUSUM 是为 abrupt changepoint 设计的，论文自身说信号是 gradual。更适合渐变信号的方法（Gaussian process regression、HMM 等）未被讨论。\n\n**W2** [warning] Ensemble proof-of-concept 处于不上不下状态\n- 具体位置：Section 5 最后一段\n- 问题：+0.5 到 +2.7pp 提升——统计显著但 practical significance 小。缺少 ablation。\n\n**W3** [warning] Abstract 信息密度过高，对首次读者不友好\n\n**W4** [warning] 标题可能过于狭窄，未覆盖 encoder-quality、temporal detection 等贡献\n\n**W5** [warning] Chan & Yong (2025) 的处理不够充分——未讨论两篇工作在层选择策略和结论上的具体异同\n\n---\n\n## 小问题\n\n- M1: Figure 1 色觉障碍友好性待改进\n- M2: Table 2 过于密集\n- M3: Section 6 (Conclusion) 约 2-3 行溢出到 page 9，正文超 8 页限制\n- M4: geiger2025causal 年份不匹配（bib=2025, JMLR=2023）\n- M5: Figure 2 标签尺寸较小\n- M6: 部分 \\cite/\\citet/\\citep 混用\n\n---\n\n## Questions for Authors\n\nQ1: 是否尝试了比 BGE-1024d 更强的 encoder？如果 HS 优势随 encoder 质量单调递减，这意味着什么？\nQ2: 层选择敏感性是否在不同有害类别间一致？\nQ3: 7 种 temporal 方法应用于 raw hidden states 还是 probe predictions？\nQ4: 5-metric audit protocol 的计算开销？\n\n---\n\n## Missing References\n\n1. SafeProbing (Zhu et al., 2026, arXiv:2601.10543) — 在 decoding 过程中 probe hidden states 检测安全信号\n2. \"A Concrete Roadmap towards Safety Cases based on Chain-of-Thought Monitoring\" (arXiv:2510.19476)\n3. \"Probing Latent Subspaces in LLM for AI Security\" (arXiv:2503.09066)\n\n---\n\n## 主要优势\n\n- S1: 统计方法论严谨（Bootstrap CI、Holm-Bonferroni、control task、BCa）\n- S2: 诚实的证据呈现，用 evidence gradient 而非 binary 结论\n- S3: 对社区有实用价值——5-metric audit protocol 提供具体最佳实践\n- S4: Appendix 质量高（18 页），可复现性极好\n- S5: 引用覆盖面广（75 篇，含大量 2025-2026 最新工作）\n\n---\n\n## 引用验证结果\n\n- 75 篇 bib entries，57 篇 online verified，15 篇 not found（多为近期 preprint 未被索引），3 篇 mismatch\n- 主要问题：geiger2025causal 年份不匹配（bib 2025 vs API 2023）\n- 0 orphan bib，0 missing bib——引用交叉检查完全通过",

[Round 1 审稿请求]:
以下是 Reviewer 1 的完整审稿意见。请逐条回应：对每个 Must-Fix/Should-Fix/Minor 问题标注 [AGREE] 同意并说明理由，或 [REBUT] 反驳并提供证据（cite 具体 Section/Table/Figure）。

---

## Reviewer 1 审稿意见：方法严谨性审查

**Overall**: 3.5 — Borderline Conference

### Must-Fix 问题

**N1** [critical] HB 校正范围严重低估实际搜索空间
- 上游探索空间为 5 metrics × 7 windows × 28-64 layers ≈ 980-2,240 configurations per model。HB 校正仅在选定的 12 个 final tests 上执行。一篇以 "researcher degrees of freedom" 为核心 narrative 的论文，其自身统计方案在这方面存在漏洞。
- 论文的 safeguards（nested CV、split-half、threshold convergence）仅覆盖 7-8B 模型；32B 模型（n=19-20）无此保护。

**N2** [critical] Abstract 中 position confound 的选择性报告
- Abstract 写 "3/4 models retain significant non-positional HS advantage"（linear residualization）。Quadratic 下仅 2/4。论文以 linear 为主、quadratic 为 sensitivity check，但 abstract 完全未提及这一敏感性。

**N3** [critical] Random-label control 的泛化不足
- Random-label control 仅在 QwQ-32B 上执行。论文据此声称 "architecture-independent geometric property"（Section 6），但缺少对 Llama 架构（R1-8B）的验证。

### Should-Fix 问题

**W1** [warning] 32B 小样本下的过度推断——R1-32B position-residualized BA +1.9pp (p=0.914) 远低于 MDE 8.6pp，但 Table 4 仍暗示实用价值
**W2** [warning] "Cross-validated" 术语的非标准使用——论文定义为 "passes nested 5-fold CV"，社区标准用法为模型选择中的 fold-based evaluation
**W3** [warning] 5-metric 分类的 post-hoc 性质——论文自述 "empirical observation, not a priori"
**W4** [warning] 单数据集（HarmThoughts）的外部效度——AdvBench 仅做 cosine volatility 验证
**W5** [warning] CP 标注在主数据集上缺 IAA——AdvBench 上 LLM IAA κ=0.606 为 moderate，CP position MAD=8.3 steps
**W6** [warning] GRU 与 LR 对比不公平——不同 split (70/30 vs 60/20/20)、不同训练协议

### Minor

- M1: Table 2 p-value 格式不一致（两组不同统计检验的 p 值混在同一表）
- M2: Section 3.3 "volatility ratio 1.67×" 未在正文解释计算方法
- M3: Figure 1 heatmap 缺少具体数值刻度
- M4: K=5 是否应 model-dependent（32B 模型步数更多）
- M5: "7-8B models" 混合指代不够精确

### Questions
Q1: 32B 模型 false discovery risk 除 threshold convergence 外有无其他量化方式？
Q2: QwQ-32B quadratic position control 下 HS 优势消失——是否意味着 HS probe 在学 position 的非线性函数？
Q3: Additive composite 被归为 threshold-based——如果 crossing-rate 权重增大会翻转吗？
Q4: K=5 对不同 trace 长度分布的模型是否同样适用？
Q5: HarmThoughts 的 CP 标注来源？Benchmark 自带 IAA 吗？

### Missing References
- Stickland & Murray (2019) "BERT and PALs"
- Liu et al. (2019) "Linguistic Knowledge and Transferability of Contextual Representations"

### Strengths
- S1: Layer selection 作为 researcher degree of freedom 对 safety probing 有直接实践价值
- S2: 异常详尽的统计检验流程
- S3: Crossing-rate bias 机制性解释完整（cosine compression → noise floor → inflated crossings 三重支撑）
- S4: 负面结果和失败案例的坦诚报告
- S5: Evidence tier 分类增强可解读性
- S6: Multi-metric ensemble 提出具体缓解方案

---

请逐条用 [AGREE] 或 [REBUT] 回应上述所有问题（Must-Fix、Should-Fix、Minor），并给出你的理由。

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
The Review Director wants me (Reviewer 2) to respond to Reviewer 1's complete review, going through each issue and marking [AGREE] or [REBUT] with evidence from the paper. Let me go through each point carefully.

Let me re-read the paper's key sections to make sure I have accurate information before responding.

Let me think through each of R1's points:

**N1**: HB correction scope underestimates search space
- R1 says the upstream exploration space is 5 metrics × 7 windows × 28-64 layers ≈ 980-2,240 configs, but HB correction only applies to the final 12 tests.


- This is a legitimate statistical concern about the gap between the actual exploration space and what the correction accounts for. The paper does mention nested CV for the 7-8B model but not for 32B, so there's an inconsistency worth flagging. I should [AGREE] with this point.

**N2**: Selective reporting of position confound in Abstract
- The abstract highlights "3/4 models retain significant non-positional HS advantage" using linear residualization, but quadratic residualization only shows this for 2/4 models.
- This is a fair observation about how the abstract emphasizes the stronger result without mentioning the weaker one. I should [AGREE] that the abstract could be more balanced in presenting both findings.

**N3**: Random-label control only on QwQ-32B
- The paper claims an "architecture-independent geometric property" but only validates this claim on a single architecture, which doesn't actually support the generality being claimed.
- I should [AGREE] with this critique.

**W1**: 32B small sample over-inference
- The R1-32B results have very small sample sizes (n=19-20) with a position-residualized boost of +1.9pp at p=0.914, well below the minimum detectable effect of 8.6pp.
- The paper does label these as "exploratory," which provides some acknowledgment, but Table 4 still presents them in a way that could overstate their reliability. I should [AGREE] with this concern while noting the paper does offer some caveats.

**W2**: "Cross-validated" non-standard usage
- The paper uses "cross-validated" to describe results that pass nested CV in a way that doesn't align with standard statistical terminology.
- In probing literature this usage is fairly common, so it's more of a clarity issue than a fundamental misuse. I'll [AGREE PARTIALLY] that it could be clearer without treating it as severely problematic.

**W3**: 5-metric classification is post-hoc
- The paper itself acknowledges this is an "empirical observation, not a priori," so the post-hoc nature is transparent.
- I should [AGREE] that this does limit the strength of the contribution, though the authors are upfront about it.

**W4**: Single dataset external validity
- The paper only uses HarmThoughts for the main analysis, with AdvBench appearing only for cosine volatility testing.
- This mirrors a concern I raised in my own review, so I'll [AGREE] that broader evaluation would strengthen the work.

**W5**: CP annotation lacks IAA on main dataset
- AdvBench shows moderate agreement (κ=0.606) and position variability (MAD=8.3 steps), but the main dataset lacks this quality assessment.
- I should [AGREE] this is a legitimate concern about annotation reliability.

**W6**: GRU vs LR comparison unfair
- The two models use different data splits and training procedures, making direct comparison problematic.
- I'll [AGREE] this is a valid experimental design issue, though it's somewhat tangential to the paper's core contribution since GRU functions mainly as a baseline.

**Minor issues:**
- Table 2 p-value formatting looks reasonable, so [AGREE]. The volatility ratio of 1.67× should be explained in the text rather than left implicit, so [AGREE]. Figure 1 needs a value scale on its axis, which I also noticed, so [AGREE].
- K=5 being model-dependent is a fair point I can partially [AGREE] with—it could be addressed in the limitations section.
- The "7-8B models" phrasing is inconsistent and could be clearer, so [AGREE].

Now reconsidering where I might push back: For N1, the paper does employ nested cross-validation to guard against overfitting during layer selection, with Holm-Bonferroni correction applied to the final hypothesis tests rather than the exploration phase. This is standard practice—you select your configuration via CV, then run corrected tests on that selection. The real question is whether CV alone suffices, and R1 has a point about the gap between exploration and correction. The 32B model lacking this protection is a legitimate gap I should [AGREE] with, though with some nuance.

Regarding N3, I need to look more carefully at what the paper actually claims about this point.

For N2, I'm noticing something important: the abstract reports "3/4 models retain significant non-positional HS advantage" using linear residualization, but under quadratic it drops to 2/4. In regression-based position control, linear is the standard approach with quadratic as a sensitivity check, so reporting the main result in the abstract is normal. But when a sensitivity check flips the conclusion direction like this, it deserves at least a flag in the abstract. That's selective reporting I should [AGREE] with.

On W2, "cross-validated" in probing contexts typically means the accuracy was evaluated via cross-validation, though the paper uses it more loosely to mean the finding survives cross-validation. That's informal but common usage, so I'll [AGREE] with reduced severity.

For W6, the training protocols are actually problematic: the GRU uses a 70/30 split while LR uses 60/20/20, meaning LR has less training data but includes a validation set while GRU doesn't. Since the paper uses LR as the main method and compares against GRU as a baseline, this difference makes the comparison unfair. I should [AGREE] with this one.
```

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
Now I have enough information to respond to each of R1's points. Let me also check a couple more appendix pages for the GRU comparison and position control details.

Actually, let me look at what I have:

Page 15: Appendix B (Layer Sweep results), B.1 Random-Label Decomposition Control (on QwQ-32B only), Table 7 showing random-label control results, C Representation Compression Analysis, Table 8

Page 16: Cross-dataset evidence (AdvBench), C.1 AdvBench Boundary Condition, Table 9, D Holm-Bonferroni Step-Down Correction, Table 10


Page 20: H Control Task Methodology (Hewitt & Liang 2019 framework), I Oracle Upper Bound Analysis

Now I'm working through R1's critique point by point. For the HB correction scope issue, R1 is claiming the upstream space has roughly 980-2,240 possible configurations across metrics, windows, and layers, but the Holm-Bonferroni correction only addresses 12 final tests. Let me check what the paper actually says about the evaluation protocol and threshold selection.

Looking at the relevant sections, the paper does acknowledge this limitation explicitly—it states that while HB correction covers the final 12 model-metric comparisons, it doesn't account for the cumulative researcher degrees of freedom in earlier decisions like layer selection, metric choice, window size, and evidence tier. The Limitations section even notes that Type I error may be underestimated because of this. So R1's statistical concern is valid, but the paper already addresses it transparently.

For the 32B results, the paper labels them as "exploratory" and notes that the safeguards only apply to the 7-8B models, so there's some acknowledgment of the difference in rigor there too.

Now looking at the selective reporting concern in the Abstract—it claims "3/4 models retain significant non-positional HS advantage," but I need to check whether this holds under different statistical controls. The paper shows this result on page 7, but I should verify whether the count changes depending on which correction method is applied. QwQ-32B loses significance under the quadratic model while the 7-8B models retain it, so the count drops from 3 out of 4 models to 2 out of 4. R1's point stands—the Abstract should acknowledge this sensitivity to model specification rather than only reporting the linear result. Now looking at the random-label control experiment, which was only applied to QwQ-32B.

The paper claims an "architecture-independent geometric property," but the random-label control that isolates geometry from safety information only ran on QwQ-32B. R1 is right that testing this control on a Llama model like R1-8B would strengthen the claim. That said, the full layer sweep does span both Qwen and Llama architectures across all four models, so the broader finding isn't entirely unsupported—but using a single architecture's random-label control to justify "architecture-independent" is overreaching. I'll concede this point with the caveat that the layer sweep itself covers both families.

For the R1-32B results, the paper does acknowledge these as exploratory with small samples (n=19-20), and the position-residualized effect shows weak statistical support (p=0.914). The issue is that Table 4 presents them in the same format as the more robust 7-8B findings, which could mislead readers about the reliability difference. The paper flags the limitation but the table layout doesn't visually distinguish it.

Now I'm checking how the paper actually uses "cross-validated" in the discussion section around the five-metric ablation and the crossing-rate finding.

Looking at the specific language on page 2 and in the abstract, the paper uses "cross-validated" to mean the findings were confirmed through nested cross-validation, which is actually fairly standard ML terminology. The nested 5-fold CV results showing the hidden-state precision advantage across 10/10 folds with 13-25% shrinkage is a legitimate validation approach. The terminology isn't ideal but it's not as misleading as the reviewer suggests—I'd say the paper's usage is acceptable, though slightly imprecise.

For the post-hoc classification concern, the paper is transparent about this being an empirical observation rather than a predetermined design choice in Section 3.3, which mitigates the issue somewhat. I agree it's a limitation, but the authors acknowledge it honestly.

The single dataset limitation is real—only HarmThoughts for the main analysis, with AdvBench used narrowly for cosine volatility verification. This is a fair criticism.

On the CP annotation inter-annotator agreement, the paper acknowledges on page 9 that the step-level CP annotations come from manual inspection of reasoning chains, and the mean CP position among jailbreaks is around 1.4 according to the AdvBench section on page 16.

Now looking at R1's concern about the GRU versus logistic regression comparison—they're pointing out that different experimental splits and training protocols were used. The paper reports balanced accuracy, precision, pre-CP false positive rate, coverage, and lead time in Section 3.6, explicitly excluding F1, AUROC, and MCC for specific methodological reasons. I need to check what the actual split configurations were in Section 4.1 to see if the comparison is truly unfair.

The paper uses 60/20/20 as the primary split and 70/30 as secondary, but I'm not finding explicit details about the GRU training protocol in the main text—this might be in the appendix. R1's concern about inconsistent splits between methods is valid and makes the comparison less clean, though this is about baseline methods rather than the core contribution. I'll acknowledge this as a fair point.

For the minor issues, the p-value formatting in Table 2 is something I noticed as well, so I'll agree with that too.

_source line 185 · 2026-05-20T16:19:06.389Z_

---

## Reviewer 2 对 Reviewer 1 审稿意见的逐条回应

### Must-Fix 问题

**[AGREE] R1-N1: HB 校正范围低估搜索空间**

同意这是一个实质性的统计方法论问题。论文自身在 Section 7 (Limitations, page 9) 已明确承认："HB correction covers the final 12 model-metric tests but not the cumulative researcher degrees of freedom in layer, metric, window, and evidence tier selection; Type I error may be underestimated"。这表明作者意识到了问题，但仅在 Limitations 中一笔带过不够——一篇以 "researcher degrees of freedom" 为核心叙事的论文，其自身统计方案应更严格地处理这个问题（如 permutation-based family-wise correction 覆盖全搜索空间，或至少报告 exploration-adjusted p-values）。

R1 关于 32B 模型缺乏 nested CV 保护的补充论点也成立。论文将 32B 标为 "exploratory"（Section 4.1 footnote），但这个标签淹没在密集的统计数字中，读者容易忽略。

**[AGREE] R1-N2: Abstract 中 position confound 的选择性报告**

同意。Section 4.2 (page 7) 明确记载：linear residualization 下 3/4 模型保留显著 HS 优势，但 quadratic 下 QwQ-32B 的优势 "is fully absorbed by the quadratic term (table 29)"，降至 2/4。Abstract 只报告 3/4 的 linear 结果，未提及 quadratic 的敏感性。这对 QwQ-32B 尤其关键，因为它是论文中唯一的 32B 模型之一且做了 random-label control——如果其 HS 优势可能被 position 的非线性函数解释，这直接影响 Contribution 2 的可信度。

补充：这与我自己的 N2 (encoder-quality 发现削弱 HS 叙事) 形成交叉印证——HS 优势同时对 encoder 选择和 position control 方法敏感，进一步暗示其 robustness 不足。

**[AGREE] R1-N3: Random-label control 泛化不足**

同意。Section B.1 (Appendix, page 15) 的 random-label control 仅在 QwQ-32B（Qwen 架构）上执行。Section 6 (Conclusion) 声称 "architecture-independent geometric property"，但这需要至少在 Llama 架构（R1-8B）上复现才能成立。

补充一个缓解因素：完整的 layer sweep（Section 4.2, Table 2）确实覆盖了两种架构（Qwen 和 Llama），且 crossing-rate shallow bias 模式在所有 4 个模型上均可观察到（Figure 1）。因此 "architecture-independent" 的 claim 有来自主实验的间接支持，但 random-label control 作为因果论证的关键证据，确实需要跨架构验证。R1 的批评在逻辑严格性上完全成立。

---

### Should-Fix 问题

**[AGREE] R1-W1: 32B 小样本过度推断**

同意。R1-32B 的 n=19-20 远低于可检测 meaningful effect 的样本量。Section 7 (page 9) 报告 MDE 为 8.6pp，而 position-residualized BA 仅 +1.9pp (p=0.914)——统计功效严重不足。论文将 32B 标为 "exploratory"，但 Table 4 (page 7) 以与 7-8B 结果相同的格式呈现 R1-32B 数据，包括 "limited early identification with narrow coverage" 等描述性语言，可能让读者误以为这些是有统计支持的发现。建议 Table 4 增加视觉区分（如灰色底色）标明 exploratory 结果。

**[AGREE with reduced severity] R1-W2: "Cross-validated" 术语**

部分同意。论文在 Abstract 和 Section 5 中用 "cross-validated" 表示 "the finding passes/survives nested 5-fold CV"（如 page 5: "Nested 5-fold CV confirms the HS precision advantage for both 7-8B models (10/10 folds; section W.1)"）。这与 ML 社区中 "cross-validated accuracy" 的标准用法有语义偏差但不构成误导——读者从上下文可以理解其含义。建议改为 "validated via nested CV" 以减少歧义即可，严重性低于 R1 的评级。

**[AGREE] R1-W3: 5-metric 分类的 post-hoc 性质**

同意。Section 3.3 (page 4) 坦诚声明 "an empirical observation from metric disagreement, not an a priori design choice"，这一透明性值得肯定。但 post-hoc 分类确实削弱了 CR-composite vs. threshold-based 二分法作为 contribution 的力度——如果换一组度量或换一种分类标准，二分法是否仍然成立？论文未提供这方面的 robustness check。

**[AGREE] R1-W4: 单数据集外部效度**

完全同意，这也是我在 R2-Q2 中提出的问题。HarmThoughts 是唯一的主实验数据集；AdvBench 仅用于 cosine volatility 验证（Section C.1, page 16）而非完整 layer selection replication。Section 7 (Limitations) 承认 "we do not study results by category"，但连跨数据集的层选择一致性都未验证，外部效度存疑。

**[AGREE] R1-W5: CP 标注缺 IAA**

同意。HarmThoughts 的 CP 标注来自 "manual inspection of reasoning chains"（Section 7, page 9），但未报告标注者间一致性（IAA）。AdvBench 的 IAA κ=0.606 仅为 moderate，CP position MAD=8.3 steps——这意味着对同一 trace，不同标注者可能给出差异约 8 步的 CP 位置。对于以 CP 为锚点的分类任务，这种标注噪声直接影响 label quality 和下游结论的可靠性。

**[AGREE with nuance] R1-W6: GRU 与 LR 对比不公平**

同意原则上的方法论关切：不同 split 和训练协议使直接比较受污染。但需指出这个问题的严重性取决于 GRU 在论文论证中的角色。GRU 是 Section 3.4 中 7 种 negative-result baseline 之一，论文的核心 claim 并不依赖 GRU vs. LR 的定量比较。因此这是一个 should-fix 级别的方法论清洁度问题，但不影响核心结论。

---

### Minor

**[AGREE] R1-M1**: Table 2 混合两种不同统计检验的 p 值确实增加误读风险，建议拆分或增加明确标注。

**[AGREE] R1-M2**: Section 3.3 "volatility ratio 1.67×" 在正文中出现但未解释计算方法。我在 page 9 (Limitations) 看到 "the geometric precondition (shallow-layer cosine compression)...volatility ratio 1.67×" 再次被引用，但计算细节应在首次出现时给出或引用具体 Appendix section。

**[AGREE] R1-M3**: Figure 1 heatmap 确实缺少具体数值刻度。热图需要 colorbar 以让读者量化跨模型的绝对差异，而非仅观察相对模式。这也与我的 R2-M1（色觉障碍友好性）形成交叉。

**[AGREE with reduced severity] R1-M4**: K=5 (window sizes) 是否应随模型 trace 长度变化是合理问题。Section 9 (page 9) 提到 "K=5 was selected on the R1-8B validation set and held fixed"——这意味着 32B 模型（trace 通常更长）可能需要不同的 K 值。但论文将 K 视为固定超参数在 R1-8B 上选定后泛化到其他模型，这在方法论上是 defensible 的（防止 per-model tuning），只需更明确地讨论 trade-off。

**[AGREE] R1-M5**: "7-8B models" 指代 R1-8B (DeepSeek-R1-Distill-Llama-8B) 和 OT-7B (OpenThinker-7B)，两者参数量不同（8B vs 7B）且架构不同（Llama vs ？），统称 "7-8B" 确实不够精确。

---

现在将回应发送给 Review Director。
